<div class='question question'>

Work out $5502 \div 6$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 5502 \div 6 \\\\
&= \dfrac{5502}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{917 \times 6}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{917 \times \cancel{6}}{\cancel{6}} \\\\
&= 917
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$917$

</div>
</div>

&= \dfrac{5502}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{917 \times 6}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{917 \times \cancel{6}}{\cancel{6}} \\\\
&= 917
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Circle the number in the list below which is closest in value to $1$.

$0.993   \quad 1.006  \quad 1.1  \quad 0.94  \quad 0.99$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's calculate the difference between the numbers and $1$.

| Number   |     Difference      |
|:--------:|:-------------------:|
|  $0.993$ | $1 - 0.993 = 0.007$ |
|  $1.006$ | $1.006 - 1 = 0.006$ |
|  $1.1$   | $1.1 - 1 = 0.1$     |
|  $0.94$  | $1 - 0.94 = 0.06$   |
|  $0.99$  | $1 - 0.99 = 0.01$   |

Smallest difference $= 0.006$

Number closest in value $= 1.006$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.006$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's calculate the difference between the numbers and $1$.

|  $1.1$   | $1.1 - 1 = 0.1$     |
|  $0.94$  | $1 - 0.94 = 0.06$   |
|  $0.99$  | $1 - 0.99 = 0.01$   |
<emptyline>
Smallest difference $= 0.006$
<emptyline>
Number closest in value $= 1.006$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Work out $\dfrac{5}{8}$ of $48$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{5}{8} \text{ of } 48 \\\\
&= \dfrac{5}{8} \times 48 \\\\
&= \dfrac{5 \times 48}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{5 \times 6 \times 8}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{5 \times 6 \times \cancel{8}}{\cancel{8}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 6  \\\\
&= 30
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

&= \dfrac{5 \times 6 \times 8}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{5 \times 6 \times \cancel{8}}{\cancel{8}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 6  \\\\
&= 30
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Write a number in the box to complete the number pattern below.

$
\boxed{\phantom{\ 55.5 \ }} \quad 
\boxed{\ 20.0 \ } \quad  
\boxed{\ 20.2 \ } \quad  
\boxed{\ 20.4 \ }
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by adding $0.2$ to the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+0.2)}} 
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+0.2)}}  
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+0.2)}}   
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+0.2)}}  \\
\ldots, && 20.0, &&  20.2, &&  20.4
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Missing number}      &= 20.0 - 0.2 \\\\
                        &= 19.8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19.8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>

&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+0.2)}}  \\

\end{matrix}
$
<emptyline>
$

                        &= 19.8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
8 \times 7 + a &= 87 \\\\
56 + a &= 87 \\\\
a      &= 87 - 56 \\\\
a      &= 31
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$31$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$(\boxed{\phantom{5555}} + 7) \times 6 = 54$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 7) \times 6 &= 54 \\\\
a + 7            &= \dfrac{54}{6} \\\\
a + 7            &= \dfrac{9 \times 6}{6} \\\\
a + 7            &= \dfrac{9 \times \cancel 6}{\cancel 6} \\\\
a + 7            &= 9 \\\\
a                &= 9 - 7 \\\\
a                &= 2 
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $=2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>

a                &= 2 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>

<div class='workings'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
14.4 \times a  &= 144000 \\\\

a              &= \dfrac{144000}{14.4} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac{144000 \times 10}{14.4 \times 10} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac{1440000}{144} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac{144 \times 10000}{144} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac{\cancel {144} \times 10000}{\cancel {144}} \\\\
a              &= 10000
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $=10000$

</div>

<p>1543018F-C932-4ED3-845B-8C42976D5181</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1:1:12</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='question question'>

Lucy is thinking of two numbers.

The sum of the two numbers is $17$. 

When she multiplies them together, the result is $66$.

What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first numbers $= a$ 

Let the second number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b               &= 17 \\\\
a \times b          &= 66 
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.

Lets find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

a. Both the numbers should be smaller than $17$.

b. The product of the $2$ numbers is $66$.

c. The sum of the $2$ numbers is $17$.


Factors of $66 = 1, 6, 11, 66$

Factor pairs of $66$ which are less than $17 = (6, 11)$

| Numbers   |  Multiplication  |  Sum  |
|:-------:  |:---------------: |:----: |
| $6, 11$   | $66$             | $17$  |

A quick check shows that $6$ and $11$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two number $= 6$ and $11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6 \text{ and } 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first numbers $= a$ 
<emptyline>
Let the second number $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b               &= 17 \\\\
a \times b          &= 66 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.
<emptyline>
Lets find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.
<emptyline>
a. Both the numbers should be smaller than $17$.
<emptyline>
b. The product of the $2$ numbers is $66$.
<emptyline>
c. The sum of the $2$ numbers is $17$.
<emptyline>

Factors of $66 = 1, 6, 11, 66$
<emptyline>
Factor pairs of $66$ which are less than $17 = (6, 11)$
<emptyline>
| Numbers   |  Multiplication  |  Sum  |
|:-------:  |:---------------: |:----: |
| $6, 11$   | $66$             | $17$  |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $6$ and $11$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. rtq_abbr_md_quadratic_note*
<emptyline>
The two number $= 6$ and $11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1:1:14</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

In a shop, $3$ stickers cost $40 \ \text{pence}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many stickers can be bought for $\pounds 2.40?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of stickers for 1p}            &= \dfrac {3} {40} \\\\
\text{Number of stickers for \pounds 2.40}  &= \dfrac {3} {40} \times 2.40 \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 240} {40} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 40 \times 6} {40} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {40} \times 6} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
                                            &= 3 \times 6 \\\\
                                            &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of stickers $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
40 \ \text{p} : 3 \ \text{stickers}               &= 2.40 \times 100 \ \text{p} : a \ \text{stickers} \\\\
\dfrac{40} {3}                                  &= \dfrac {2.40 \times 100} {a} \\\\
40 \times a                                     &= 2.40 \times 100 \times 3 \\\\
40 \times a                                     &= 240 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {240 \times 3} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {40 \times 6 \times 3} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times 6 \times 3} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
a                                               &= 6 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Number of stickers $= 18$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cost}                             &:& \text{stickers} \\\\
= & 40 \ \text{p}                             &:& 3 \\\\
= & 40 \times 6                             &:& 3 \times 6 \\\\
= & 240 \ \text{p}                            &:& 18 \\\\
= & \pounds 2.40                            &:& 18
\end{array}
$

Number of stickers $= 18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of stickers for 1p}            &= \dfrac {3} {40} \\\\
\text{Number of stickers for \pounds 2.40}  &= \dfrac {3} {40} \times 2.40 \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 240} {40} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 40 \times 6} {40} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {40} \times 6} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
                                            &= 3 \times 6 \\\\
                                            &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of stickers $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
40 \ \text{p} : 3 \ \text{stickers}               &= 2.40 \times 100 \ \text{p} : a \ \text{stickers} \\\\
\dfrac{40} {3}                                  &= \dfrac {2.40 \times 100} {a} \\\\
40 \times a                                     &= 2.40 \times 100 \times 3 \\\\
40 \times a                                     &= 240 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {240 \times 3} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {40 \times 6 \times 3} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times 6 \times 3} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
a                                               &= 6 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of stickers $= 18$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cost}                             &:& \text{stickers} \\\\
= & 40 \ \text{p}                             &:& 3 \\\\
= & 40 \times 6                             &:& 3 \times 6 \\\\
= & 240 \ \text{p}                            &:& 18 \\\\
= & \pounds 2.40                            &:& 18
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of stickers $= 18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much  would $33$ stickers cost? *(Give your answer in pounds.)*

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 sticker}      &= \dfrac {40} {3} \ \text{p} \\\\
\text{Cost of 33 stickers}    &= \dfrac {40} {3} \times 33 \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times 33} {3} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times 3 \times 11} {3} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times \cancel 3 \times 11} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                              &= 40 \times 11 \\\\
                              &= 440 \ \text{p} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 4.40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of stickers $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{stickers} : 40 \ \text{p}               &= 33 \ \text{stickers} : a \ \text{p} \\\\
\dfrac{3} {40}                                  &= \dfrac {33} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 33 \times 40 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {33 \times 40} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 11 \times 40} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 11 \times 40} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 11 \times 40 \\\\
a                                               &= 440 \ \text{p} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 4.40
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of stickers $= \pounds 4.4$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{stickers}                         &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 40 \ \text{p} \\\\
= & 3 \times 11                             &:& 40 \times 11 \ \text{p} \\\\
= & 3 \times 11                             &:& 440 \ \text{p} \\\\
= & 33                                      &:& \pounds 4.40
\end{array}
$

Cost of stickers $= \pounds 4.4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                              &= \dfrac {40 \times 33} {3} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times 3 \times 11} {3} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times \cancel 3 \times 11} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                              &= 40 \times 11 \\\\

<div class='question question'>

Kitty is $8 \ \text{years}$ and $5 \ \text{months}$ old.

Rover is $5 \ \text{years}$ and $8 \ \text{months}$ younger than Kitty.

How old is Rover?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Rover's age}     &= \text {Kitty's age} - 5 \ \text {years} \ 8 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 8 \ \text {years} \ 5 \ \text {months} - 5 \ \text {years} \ 8 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 2 \ \text {years} \ 9 \ \text {months}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 \ \text {years} \ 9 \ \text {months}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Rover's age}     &= \text {Kitty's age} - 5 \ \text {years} \ 8 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 8 \ \text {years} \ 5 \ \text {months} - 5 \ \text {years} \ 8 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 2 \ \text {years} \ 9 \ \text {months}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2 \ \text {years} \ 9 \ \text {months}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Which input would give an output of $23$ from Machine $A$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the input be $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 3 + 5 &= 23 \\\\
3a + 5         &= 23 \\\\
3a             &= 23 - 5 \\\\
3a             &= 18 \\\\
a              &= \dfrac{18}{3} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac{6 \times 3}{3} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac{6 \times \cancel 3}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a              &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>

a              &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which input would give the same output from both machines?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the input $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 3 + 5 &= a \times 5 + 3 \\\\
3a + 5         &= 5a + 3 \\\\
5a - 3a        &= 5 - 3 \\\\
2a             &= 2 \\\\
a              &= \dfrac{2}{2} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac{\cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a              &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the input $= a$

$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much cheese is needed to make $4$ portions of cheesy pasta?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of cheese needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
8 \ \text{portions} : 150 \ \text{grams}            &= 4 \ \text{portions} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{8} {150}                                 &= \dfrac {4} {a} \\\\
8 \times a                                      &= 4 \times 150 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 150} {8} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 75} {2 \times 4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2 \times 75} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 75 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Cheese needed $= 75 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{portions}                         &:& \text{cheese} \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& 150 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {150} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 4                              &:& \dfrac {150 \times 4} {8} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 75 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& 75 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Cheese needed $= 75 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of cheese needed $= a$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}

\dfrac{8} {150}                                 &= \dfrac {4} {a} \\\\
8 \times a                                      &= 4 \times 150 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 150} {8} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 75} {2 \times 4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2 \times 75} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 75 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cheese needed $= 75 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>

= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {150} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 4                              &:& \dfrac {150 \times 4} {8} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 75 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& 75 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Cheese needed $= 75 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Mrs. Smith is making $20$ portions of cheesy pasta.

Complete the list below to show the amount of each ingredient which Mrs. Smith needs.



$\ldots \ldots \text{grams}$ pasta

$\ldots \ldots \text{grams}$ cheese

$\ldots \ldots \text{millilitres}$ cream

$20$ sundried tomatoes

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{portions}       &:& \text{pasta}                                                                           &:& \text{cheese}                                                                          &:& \text{cream}                                                                          &:& \text{tomatoes} \\\\
= & 8                     &:& 500 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 150 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 20 \ \text{ml}                                                                        &:& 8 \\\\
= & 1                     &:& \dfrac {500} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {150} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {20} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {8} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 20           &:& \dfrac {500 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {150 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {20 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {8 \times 20} {8} \\\\
= & 20                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 250 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 75 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 10 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 20} {\cancel 8} \\\\
= & 20                    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 250 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 10 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& 20 \\\\
= & 20                    &:& 250 \times 5                                                                           &:& 75 \times 5                                                                            &:& 10 \times 5                                                                           &:& 20 \\\\
= & 20                    &:& 1250 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 375 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 50 \ \text{ml}                                                                        &:& 20
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Pasta $=$ $1250 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Cheese $=$ $375 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Cream $=$ $50 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Tomatoes $=$ $1250$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{portions}       &:& \text{pasta}                                                                           &:& \text{cheese}                                                                          &:& \text{cream}                                                                          &:& \text{tomatoes} \\\\
= & 8                     &:& 500 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 150 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 20 \ \text{ml}                                                                        &:& 8 \\\\
= & 1                     &:& \dfrac {500} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {150} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {20} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {8} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 20           &:& \dfrac {500 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {150 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {20 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {8 \times 20} {8} \\\\
= & 20                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 250 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 75 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 10 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 20} {\cancel 8} \\\\
= & 20                    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 250 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 10 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& 20 \\\\
= & 20                    &:& 250 \times 5                                                                           &:& 75 \times 5                                                                            &:& 10 \times 5                                                                           &:& 20 \\\\
= & 20                    &:& 1250 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 375 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 50 \ \text{ml}                                                                        &:& 20
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

Rose buys a book which costs $\pounds 7.46$.

She pays for the book with a $\pounds 10$ note and receives $4$ coins as her change.

Which $4$ coins does Rose receive?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Change received}     &= 10 - 7.46 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 2.54 \\\\
\text {Coins recieved}      &= \pounds 2 + 50 \ \text{p} + 2 \ \text{p} + 2 \ \text{p}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2, \ 50 \ \text{p}, \ 2 \ \text{p}, \ 2 \ \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Change received}     &= 10 - 7.46 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 2.54 \\\\
\text {Coins recieved}      &= \pounds 2 + 50 \ \text{p} + 2 \ \text{p} + 2 \ \text{p}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1:1:19</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

talgebra
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

Patrick has made $\dfrac{3}{4}$ of the designs in his origami book.

If he has made $24$ designs, how many are there altogether in his book?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of designs $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{3}{4} \times a            &= 24 \\\\
a                                &= 24 \times \dfrac{4}{3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac{24 \times 4}{3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac{8 \times 3 \times 4}{3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac{8 \times \cancel 3 \times 4}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                &= 8 \times 4 \\\\
a                                &= 32 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of designs $=a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{3}{4} \times a            &= 24 \\\\
a                                &= 24 \times \dfrac{4}{3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac{24 \times 4}{3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac{8 \times 3 \times 4}{3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac{8 \times \cancel 3 \times 4}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                &= 8 \times 4 \\\\
a                                &= 32 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$32$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1:1:20</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

On Mark's plate, there are $36$ letters of Alphabet Spaghetti.

He uses $7$ of these to spell $\text{TERRIER}$, and $5$ to spell $\text{HOUND}$.

He eats the remaining letters.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of $36$ letters does Mark eat?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of letters eaten}        &= \dfrac {\text{Number of letters eaten}} {\text{Total letters}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {36 - (7 + 5)} {36} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {36 - 12} {36} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {24} {36} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {12 \times 2} {12 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 2} {\cancel {12} \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of letters eaten}        &= \dfrac {\text{Number of letters eaten}} {\text{Total letters}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {36 - (7 + 5)} {36} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {36 - 12} {36} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {24} {36} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {12 \times 2} {12 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 2} {\cancel {12} \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2} {3}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {2} {3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Mark has now the words $\text{TERRIER}$ and $\text{HOUND}$ on his plate.

What percentage of the letters now on Mark's plate can be found in the word $\text{ALSATIAN}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of letters found in ALSATIAN}    &= \dfrac {\text{Number of letters found in ALSATIAN}} {\text{Total letters in the plate}} \times 100 \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {3} {7 + 5} \times 100 \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {3} {12} \times 100 \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {3 \times 100} {12} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 25} {4 \times 3} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 25} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                                  &= 25\%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25\%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of letters found in ALSATIAN}    &= \dfrac {\text{Number of letters found in ALSATIAN}} {\text{Total letters in the plate}} \times 100 \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {3} {7 + 5} \times 100 \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {3} {12} \times 100 \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {3 \times 100} {12} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 25} {4 \times 3} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 25} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                                  &= 25\%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25\%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1:1:21</p>

<div class='question question'>

Freda Frog always does jumps of $25 \ \text{centimetres}$.

Teddy Toad always does jumps of $40 \ \text{centimetres}$.

Freda and Teddy both do $30$ jumps in a straight line in the same direction.

How much further has Teddy jumped than Freda? (Give your answer in metres.) 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (40 \times 30) - (25 \times 30) \\\\
&= 1200 - 750 \\\\
&= 450 \text{ cm} \\\\
&= \dfrac{450}{100} \text{ m} \\\\
&= 4.5 \text{ m}
\end{aligned}
$

Teddy jumped $4.5$m further than Freda.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4.5 \text{ m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= (40 \times 30) - (25 \times 30) \\\\
&= 1200 - 750 \\\\
&= 450 \text{ cm} \\\\
&= \dfrac{450}{100} \text{ m} \\\\
&= 4.5 \text{ m}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Teddy jumped $4.5$m further than Freda.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4.5 \text{ m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The total area of his shape is $120 \ \text{cm}^2$.

What is the area of one triangle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of one triangle}            &= \dfrac {\text {Total Area}} {\text {Number of triangles}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {120} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4 \times 30} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 30} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
                                        &= 30 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

                                        &= \dfrac {120} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4 \times 30} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 30} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
                                        &= 30 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1:1:30</p>

<div class='question question'>

A cat, her $4$ kittens and their cat basket altogether have a mass of $6 \dfrac{1}{2} \ \text{kilograms}$.

The cat basket has a mass of $500  \ \text{grams}$.

Each kitten has a mass of $0.6 \ \text{kilograms}$.

What is the mass of the cat?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the mass of the cat $= a$

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


Let convert all the masses into kg.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 500 \text{ gm} + 4 \times 0.6 \text{ kg}                                          &= 6.5 \text{ kg} \\\\
a + \dfrac{500}{1000} \text{ kg} + 4 \times 0.6 \text{ kg}                            &= 6.5 \text{ kg} \\\\
a + \dfrac{500}{500 \times 2} \text{ kg} + 4 \times 0.6 \text{ kg}                    &= 6.5 \text{ kg} \\\\
a + \dfrac{\cancel {500}}{\cancel {500} \times 2}  +  2.4                             &= 6.5 \\\\
a + \dfrac{1}{2}  +  2.4                                                              &= 6.5 \\\\
a + 0.5 +  2.4                                                                        &= 6.5 \\\\
a + 2.9                                                                               &= 6.5 \\\\
a                                                                                     &= 6.5 - 2.9 \\\\
a                                                                                     &= 3.6                                                          
\end{aligned}
$

Mass of the cat $= 3.6$ Kg


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3.6 \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the mass of the cat $= a$
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
Let convert all the masses into kg.
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 500 \text{ gm} + 4 \times 0.6 \text{ kg}                                          &= 6.5 \text{ kg} \\\\
a + \dfrac{500}{1000} \text{ kg} + 4 \times 0.6 \text{ kg}                            &= 6.5 \text{ kg} \\\\
a + \dfrac{500}{500 \times 2} \text{ kg} + 4 \times 0.6 \text{ kg}                    &= 6.5 \text{ kg} \\\\
a + \dfrac{\cancel {500}}{\cancel {500} \times 2}  +  2.4                             &= 6.5 \\\\
a + \dfrac{1}{2}  +  2.4                                                              &= 6.5 \\\\
a + 0.5 +  2.4                                                                        &= 6.5 \\\\
a + 2.9                                                                               &= 6.5 \\\\
a                                                                                     &= 6.5 - 2.9 \\\\
a                                                                                     &= 3.6                                                          
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Mass of the cat $= 3.6$ Kg
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3.6 \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

From which bag does he have a higher chance of picking black ball?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(black ball in bag A)} &= \dfrac {2} {9} \\\\
                              &= 0.22 \\\\
\text{P(black ball in bag B)} &= \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
                              &= 0.25
\end{aligned}
$

Higher probability = Bag $\text{B}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Bag B

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(black ball in bag A)} &= \dfrac {2} {9} \\\\
                              &= 0.22 \\\\
\text{P(black ball in bag B)} &= \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
                              &= 0.25
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Higher probability = Bag $\text{B}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Bag B
</code></pre>
</div>

<p>7C2067DD-CE34-49B3-81E9-34FB9F3C9414</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1:1:33</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Tom downloads $3$ music tracks.
The length of each track is shown in the table below.



|track    |length                            |
|:-------:|:--------------------------------:|
|track $1$|  $4 \ \text{min} \ 45 \ \text{sec}$|
|track $2$|  $5 \ \text{min} \ 5  \ \text{sec}$|
|track $3$|  $4 \ \text{min} \ 40 \ \text{sec}$|

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the total length of the $3$ tracks?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total length}    &= 4 \ \text{min } 45 \ \text{sec} + 5 \ \text{min } 5  \ \text{sec} + 4 \ \text{min } 40 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 45 \ \text{sec} + 5 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 5 \ \text{sec} + 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 40 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 240 + 45 + 300 + 5 + 240 + 40 \\\\
                        &= 870 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{sec}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total length}    &= 4 \ \text{min } 45 \ \text{sec} + 5 \ \text{min } 5  \ \text{sec} + 4 \ \text{min } 40 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 45 \ \text{sec} + 5 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 5 \ \text{sec} + 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 40 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 240 + 45 + 300 + 5 + 240 + 40 \\\\
                        &= 870 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{sec}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the mean (average) length of each track?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Using value from the answer above,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean length} &= \dfrac {870} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 290} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 290} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                    &= 290 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                    &= 4 \ \text{min} \ 50 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \ \text{min} \ 50 \ \text{sec}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Using value from the answer above,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean length} &= \dfrac {870} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 290} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 290} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                    &= 290 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                    &= 4 \ \text{min} \ 50 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \text{min} \ 50 \ \text{sec}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

There are $80$ members of the school orchestra.

$35$ of the members are boys.

$\dfrac{2}{3}$ of the girls in the orchestra play a stringed instrument.

In total, $38$ of the members do not play a stringed instrument.

Use this information to complete the table below.

|                                     | boy | girl | total |
|:-----------------------------------:|:---:|:----:|:-----:|
|     plays a stringed instrument     |     |      |       |
| does not play a stringed instrument |     |      |       |
|                total                |     |      |  $80$ |

</div>

| does not play a stringed instrument | $\green{35 - 12 = 23} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(8)}}$ |          $\green{45 - 30 = 15} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(4)}}$         |       $\green{38} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(5)}}$     |
|                total                |     $\green{35} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(1)}}$       |          $\green{80 - 35 = 45} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(2)}}$         |                 $80$                                                                |

Completed table:

| does not play a stringed instrument |$23$ | $15$ |  $38$ |
|                total                |$35$ | $45$ |  $80$ |

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jess bought a cake and a drink.

She paid $\pounds 3.90$ in total. The cake cost twice as much as the drink.

How much did the cake cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the price of the drink $=a$

Price of cake $= 2a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a          &= 3.90 \\\\
3a              &= 3.90 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{3.90}{3} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{1.30 \times 3}{3} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{1.30 \times \cancel 3}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a               &= \pounds 1.30
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Price of cake}    &= 2 \times 1.30 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 2.60 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2.60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the price of the drink $=a$
<emptyline>
Price of cake $= 2a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$

a               &= \dfrac{1.30 \times \cancel 3}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a               &= \pounds 1.30
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Price of cake}    &= 2 \times 1.30 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 2.60 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2.60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Simon bought a cup of tea and fizzy drink, paying $\pounds 2.60$ in total.

The fizzy drink cost $30  \text{p}$ more than the cup of tea.

How much did the fizzy drink cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Cost of cup of tea $= a$

Cost of fizzy drink $= a+ 30 \ \text{p} $

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


Let convert all the costs into pence.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 30 \ \text{p}          &= \pounds 2.60 \\\\
2a + 30 \ \text{p}             &= 2.60 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
2a                           &= 260 - 30 \\\\
2a                           &= 230 \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac{230}{2} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac{115 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac{115 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a                            &= 115 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of fizzy drink}   &= 115 + 30 \ \text{p} \\\\ 
                             &= 145 \ \text{p} \\\\
                             &= \pounds \dfrac{145}{100} \\\\
                             &= \pounds 1.45 
\end{aligned}
$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.45 $ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Cost of cup of tea $= a$
<emptyline>
Cost of fizzy drink $= a+ 30 \ \text{p} $
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

2a + 30 \ \text{p}             &= 2.60 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
2a                           &= 260 - 30 \\\\
2a                           &= 230 \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac{230}{2} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac{115 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac{115 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a                            &= 115 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of fizzy drink}   &= 115 + 30 \ \text{p} \\\\ 
                             &= 145 \ \text{p} \\\\
                             &= \pounds \dfrac{145}{100} \\\\
                             &= \pounds 1.45 
\end{aligned}
$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.45 $ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On Monday, Claire bought an apple and a banana. The total cost was $57  \text{p}$.

On Tuesday, Claire bought a banana and a pear. The total cost was $56  \text{p}$.

On Wednesday, Claire bought an apple and a pear. The total cost was $69  \text{p}$.

On Thursday, Claire bought an apple, a banana, and a pear.

How much did Claire spend on Thursday?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Cost of cup of an apple $= a$

Cost of banana $= b$

Cost of pear $= c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b  &= 57 \\\\
b + c  &= 56 \\\\
a + c  &= 69 
\end{aligned}
$

Adding all the three equations above, we get

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + b + c + a + c  &= ( 57 + 56 + 69 )\\\\
2a + 2b + 2c           &= 182 \\\\
2 \times (a + b + c)   &= 182 \\\\
a + b + c              &= \dfrac{182}{2} \\\\
a + b + c              &= \dfrac{91 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a + b + c              &= \dfrac{91 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a + b + c              &= 91 \text{ p}
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of an apple, a banana, and a pear $= 91 \ \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$91 \ \text{p}$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Cost of cup of an apple $= a$
<emptyline>
Cost of banana $= b$
<emptyline>
Cost of pear $= c$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b  &= 57 \\\\
b + c  &= 56 \\\\
a + c  &= 69 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Adding all the three equations above, we get
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + b + c + a + c  &= ( 57 + 56 + 69 )\\\\
2a + 2b + 2c           &= 182 \\\\
2 \times (a + b + c)   &= 182 \\\\
a + b + c              &= \dfrac{182}{2} \\\\
a + b + c              &= \dfrac{91 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a + b + c              &= \dfrac{91 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a + b + c              &= 91 \text{ p}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of an apple, a banana, and a pear $= 91 \ \text{p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$91 \ \text{p}$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1/section-1-question-39-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1/section-1-question-39-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1/section-1-question-39-00.png")

How many triangles are there in total inside this pentagon?

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1/section-1-question-39-02.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1/section-1-question-39-02.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1/section-1-question-39-02.png")

different types of triangles you find.*

<div class='question question'>

On Planet Hat, the symbol $\boxed{\wedge}$ has a special meaning in arithmetic.

$a \ \boxed{\wedge} \ b$ means multiply $a$ by $4$, and then subtract $b$.

For example, 

$
\begin{aligned} 
3  \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 2 &= 3 \times 4 - 2  \\
                        &= 12 - 3         \\
                        &= 10
\end{aligned}
$


</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOANSWER
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out $5 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 1$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 5 \times 4 - 1 \\\\
&= 20 - 1 \\\\
&= 19
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 5 \times 4 - 1 \\\\
&= 20 - 1 \\\\
&= 19
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the value of $m$ so that $m \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 6 = 26$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
m \times 4 - 6  &= 26 \\\\
4m              &= 26 + 6 \\\\
4m              &= 32 \\\\
m               &= \dfrac{32}{4} \\\\
m               &= \dfrac{8 \times 4}{4} \\\\
m               &= \dfrac{8 \times \cancel 4}{ \cancel 4} \\\\
m               &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
m \times 4 - 6  &= 26 \\\\
4m              &= 26 + 6 \\\\
4m              &= 32 \\\\
m               &= \dfrac{32}{4} \\\\
m               &= \dfrac{8 \times 4}{4} \\\\
m               &= \dfrac{8 \times \cancel 4}{ \cancel 4} \\\\
m               &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Is it possible to use the symbol twice in a calculation.

For example, 

$
\begin{aligned} 
3 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ (2 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 1) &= 3  \ \boxed{\wedge} \ (2 \times 4 - 1). \\
                                              &= 3  \ \boxed{\wedge} \  7    \\
                                              &= 3 \times 4 - 7               \\
                                              &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

Work out $5  \ \boxed{\wedge} \  (4  \ \boxed{\wedge} \  3)$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned} 
&= 5 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ (4 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 3) \\\\
&= 5  \ \boxed{\wedge} \ (4 \times 4 - 3) \\\\
&= 5  \ \boxed{\wedge} \ (16 - 3) \\\\
&= 5  \ \boxed{\wedge} \  13 \\\\
&= 5 \times 4 - 13 \\\\
&= 20 - 13 \\\\
&= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned} 
&= 5 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ (4 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 3) \\\\
&= 5  \ \boxed{\wedge} \ (4 \times 4 - 3) \\\\
&= 5  \ \boxed{\wedge} \ (16 - 3) \\\\
&= 5  \ \boxed{\wedge} \  13 \\\\
&= 5 \times 4 - 13 \\\\
&= 20 - 13 \\\\
&= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the value of $p$ so 
that $(6 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ p) \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 4 = 56$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned} 
(6 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ p) \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 4 &= 56  \\\\
(6 \times 4 - p) \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 4         &= 56  \\\\
(24 - p) \times 4 - 4                         &= 56  \\\\                                              
96 - 4p -4                                    &= 56 \\\\
92 - 4p                                       &= 56 \\\\
92                                            &= 56 + 4p \\\\
4p + 56                                       &= 92 \\\\
4p                                            &= 92 - 56 \\\\
4p                                            &= 36 \\\\
p                                             &= \dfrac{36}{4} \\\\
p                                             &= \dfrac{9 \times 4}{4} \\\\
p                                             &= \dfrac{9 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\
p                                             &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned} 
(6 \ \boxed{\wedge} \ p) \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 4 &= 56  \\\\
(6 \times 4 - p) \ \boxed{\wedge} \ 4         &= 56  \\\\
(24 - p) \times 4 - 4                         &= 56  \\\\                                              
96 - 4p -4                                    &= 56 \\\\
92 - 4p                                       &= 56 \\\\
92                                            &= 56 + 4p \\\\
4p + 56                                       &= 92 \\\\
4p                                            &= 92 - 56 \\\\
4p                                            &= 36 \\\\
p                                             &= \dfrac{36}{4} \\\\
p                                             &= \dfrac{9 \times 4}{4} \\\\
p                                             &= \dfrac{9 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\
p                                             &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

Charlie enjoys eating Nickel bars.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1/section-1-question-41-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1/section-1-question-41-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-1/section-1-question-41-00.png")

His local store has a special promotion on Nickel bars.

Charlie can exchange $3$ empty Nickel bar wrappers for a new Nickel bar.

Charlie buys $20$ Nickel bars.

Assuming that Charlie always eats any Nickel bars he has and exchanges 
the used wrappers for new bars, how many bars will he be able to eat in total?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Total nickel bars charlie bought $= 20$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of Nickel bars charlie gets after exchanging for the first time}  &= \dfrac {20} {3} \\\\
                                                                                &= 6 \text{ remainder } 2 \\\\
                                                                                &\approx 6 \\\\
\text {Number of Nickel bars charlie gets after exchanging next time}           &= \dfrac {6} {3} \\\\
                                                                                &= 2 \\\\
\text {Number of Nickel bars charlie gets after exchanging third time}          &= \dfrac {2 + 2} {3} \\\\
                                                                                &= \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
                                                                                &= 1 \text { remainder } 1 \\\\                                                                          
                                                                                &\approx 1 \\\\                                                                          
\text {Total Nickel bars charlie ate}                                           &= 20 + 6 + 2 + 1 \\\\
                                                                                &= 29
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$29$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Total nickel bars charlie bought $= 20$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of Nickel bars charlie gets after exchanging for the first time}  &= \dfrac {20} {3} \\\\
                                                                                &= 6 \text{ remainder } 2 \\\\
                                                                                &\approx 6 \\\\
\text {Number of Nickel bars charlie gets after exchanging next time}           &= \dfrac {6} {3} \\\\
                                                                                &= 2 \\\\
\text {Number of Nickel bars charlie gets after exchanging third time}          &= \dfrac {2 + 2} {3} \\\\
                                                                                &= \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
                                                                                &= 1 \text { remainder } 1 \\\\                                                                          
                                                                                &\approx 1 \\\\                                                                          
\text {Total Nickel bars charlie ate}                                           &= 20 + 6 + 2 + 1 \\\\
                                                                                &= 29
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$29$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
